> [!IMPORTANT]
> **Honest status statement.** This repository documents a **prototype** that is designed so it can grow into a field deployment. It is **not** certified to any industrial, electrical-safety, EMC, ingress-protection (IP), drinking-water or cybersecurity standard. No IP rating, "military-grade" rating or regulatory compliance is claimed. Electrical ratings that have not been decided are marked **`TBD / CONFIGURABLE`**. Figures marked **SIMULATED** come from deterministic simulators in this repository, **not** from field measurements.

> [!WARNING]
> **Measurement scope.** The system measures **water-quality parameters**: pH, turbidity, TDS, EC and temperature. It does **not** chemically identify contaminants. pH, TDS, EC and turbidity **cannot** detect arsenic, lead, mercury, fluoride, nitrate, pesticides or pathogens. Screening for a specific contaminant needs a **dedicated detection or screening subsystem** (for example a field test kit, a colorimetric or electrochemical analyser, or laboratory analysis). The architecture has a place for one (see [§ 9.6](#96-dedicated-contaminant-screening-subsystem-optional-separate)), but the prototype does not include one.

# PART A: UNDERSTANDING THE SYSTEM

## 1. Problem Statement

### 1.1 Context

Communities in remote, rural and mining-affected regions often rely on groundwater, open wells, hand pumps, storage tanks or surface streams. In these places:

- **Water-quality changes can go unnoticed** for days or weeks. Sampling is manual, infrequent and depends on someone travelling to site.
- **Grid power is unreliable or missing.** Monitoring equipment that needs mains power is impractical.
- **Connectivity is intermittent.** Cellular coverage may be weak, congested or absent for hours.
- **Maintenance visits are rare.** Equipment must keep running, and report its own health, between visits.
- **Data is hard to trust.** Paper logs and single "SAFE / UNSAFE" indicators can't be audited, can't show *why* a decision was made, and can be edited after the fact.
- **Mining activity** can change water chemistry, for example through acid mine drainage that affects pH and dissolved solids, and through suspended sediment that affects turbidity. Changes in these general parameters can be an *early indication* that a more specific investigation is needed.

### 1.2 What goes wrong with typical student IoT prototypes

| Typical prototype behaviour | Field consequence |
|---|---|
| Sends data straight to the cloud and stores nothing locally | Every connectivity outage loses data permanently |
| Stores only "SAFE / UNSAFE" | No traceability; the decision can't be re-evaluated when thresholds change |
| Uses hard-coded thresholds of unknown origin | Wrong decisions presented with false confidence |
| Claims that TDS/pH "detects arsenic" | Scientifically false and potentially dangerous |
| Lets anyone edit the database | Records can be falsified with no trace |
| Assumes a USB power bank or mains supply | Stops working after hours or days in the field |
| No self-diagnostics | A disconnected sensor reports a plausible-looking constant value for weeks |
| Breadboard in a plastic lunch box | Corrosion, condensation and loose wires within weeks outdoors |

### 1.3 Problem definition

> **Design a monitoring platform that continuously collects water-quality parameter data from verified sensors, keeps every raw reading traceably, checks every reading against documented rules, keeps working without the internet or the grid, reports its own health, and gives an on-site engineer a simple, trustworthy view, without making claims the hardware can't support.**

### 1.4 Stakeholders

| Stakeholder | Needs | How the system serves them |
|---|---|---|
| On-site engineer / technician | Clear current state; what to do next; alerts that can be trusted | Simple dashboard, local buzzer/LED alerts, guided maintenance actions |
| Service engineer | Deep diagnostics without guesswork | Separate industrial debugger |
| Supervisor / programme manager | Historical, auditable records across sites | Firebase history, audit log, exports |
| Community / water users | Early warning that the water needs further investigation | Local alert; escalation path to authorities (process, not automated diagnosis) |
| Administrator | Control over configuration and access | Role-based access, audited configuration changes |
| Researchers / auditors | Trustworthy raw data | Raw values preserved, hash-chained, verification reasons stored |

### 1.5 Scope

| In scope (prototype) | Out of scope (prototype) |
|---|---|
| pH, turbidity, TDS, EC, temperature monitoring | Chemical identification of specific contaminants |
| Rule-based verification of data quality | Declaring water "potable" or "certified safe" |
| Offline storage and later synchronisation | Real-time guaranteed delivery over cellular |
| Solar + wind hybrid charging into one battery | Guaranteed operation in every weather condition |
| Engineer dashboard and service debugger | Consumer mobile app |
| Local audible/visual alerts | Automatic shut-off of community water supply (future, needs a safety review) |
| Purification-stage *monitoring hooks* (e.g. pre/post filter readings) | Design of a certified purification plant |

> **About "Purification" in the project name.** The platform monitors water around a purification stage, for example with sensor sets before and after a filter, and exposes a low-priority load channel (P4) for purification auxiliaries such as a small pump or UV-lamp controller. It does **not** claim to make water potable. Any purification hardware must be specified, validated and approved separately.

## 2. Solution Overview

### 2.1 Core concept

```text
WATER SOURCE
    ↓
VERIFIED SENSORS            pH · Turbidity · TDS · EC · Temperature
    ↓
ESP32 FIELD NODE            acquisition, calibration, node health, modes, local alert
    ↓
RAW SENSOR DATA             JSON frame: node_id, sensor_ids, timestamp, seq, raw values
    ↓
RASPBERRY PI EDGE GATEWAY   receiver, timestamping, schema check
    ↓
DATA VALIDATION + LOCAL STORAGE   verification engine, append-only SQLite, outbox queue
    ↓
FIREBASE CLOUD              Auth, Firestore, Security Rules, Cloud Functions
    ↓
ENGINEER DASHBOARD          simple UI  (+ separate INDUSTRIAL DEBUGGER)
```

### 2.2 Power concept

```text
SOLAR ENERGY  +  WIND ENERGY
        ↓
HYBRID RENEWABLE POWER MANAGEMENT
        ↓
BATTERY STORAGE
        ↓
ESP32 + SENSORS + RASPBERRY PI + COMMUNICATION
```

### 2.3 Key capabilities

| # | Capability | Summary | Section |
|:-:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Verified deployment | 12-gate commissioning checklist before a node is marked `DEPLOYED / VERIFIED` | [§42](#42-verified-sensor-deployment) |
| 2 | Raw-first data | Original readings stored unchanged, with IDs, timestamps, mode, power and comms status | [§10](#10-raw-data-model-preserve-everything) |
| 3 | Documented verification | 19 rule IDs across 11 rule families; every result records *which* rule fired and *why* | [§12](#12-data-verification-engine) |
| 4 | Tamper resistance | Append-only tables, hash chain, role-based access, correction-by-audit-event | [§13](#13-data-integrity--tamper-resistance) |
| 5 | Offline-first | Local queue, idempotent sync, deterministic record IDs; no duplicates | [§15](#15-offline-first-operation) |
| 6 | Three operating modes | NORMAL (~5 min), LOW-POWER/BURST, EMERGENCY (high rate, local-first) | [§14](#14-operating-modes) |
| 7 | Hybrid renewable power | Solar and wind, each through its own controller, into a shared battery | [§6](#6-power-flow--hybrid-renewable-architecture) |
| 8 | Energy awareness | Measured V/I per source, load-priority shedding, hysteresis-based mode switching | [§22](#22-power-failover--load-priority) |
| 9 | Simple dashboard | Five tabs: Live, Nodes, Power, Alerts, History | [§18](#18-industrial-engineer-dashboard) |
| 10 | Industrial debugger | Service tool with sensor/node/edge/comms/power/verification panels and exportable diagnostic reports | [§19](#19-industrial-debugger) |
| 11 | Testability | Every test case written as INPUT → PROCESS → EXPECTED → ACTUAL → PASS/FAIL | [§46](#46-comprehensive-test-plan) |

### 2.4 What makes this "field-grade" rather than "demo-grade"

| Concern | Demo-grade approach | This project's approach |
|---|---|---|
| Data loss on outage | Accepted | Designed out: local append-only store plus outbox |
| Trust in data | Implicit | Explicit: every record carries a verification result and a reason |
| Editing history | Possible | Blocked by Security Rules and DB triggers; corrections are audit events |
| Sensor failure | Silent | Detected: missing, out-of-range, stuck and inconsistent rules |
| Power | Assumed | Measured, budgeted, prioritised, mode-switched |
| Clock | Assumed correct | Validated: skew, monotonicity, RTC/NTP/GSM time sources |
| Configuration | Hard-coded | Versioned config files; thresholds `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` |
| Claims | Aspirational | Bounded: explicit limitations section and SIMULATED labels |

---

## 3. Engineering Principles & Honest Claims Policy

### 3.1 Ten engineering principles

1. **Raw data is sacred.** Never overwrite, never delete, never "clean" a raw reading in place.
2. **Verification is classification, not truth.** The engine classifies readings against documented rules. It does not decide whether a chemical measurement is scientifically "true".
3. **Every rule is documented.** A rule that isn't in the [Rule Catalogue](#124-verification-rule-catalogue) doesn't exist in code.
4. **No invented thresholds.** Water-quality attention bands are disabled by default and must come from a verified source or an explicit project decision, recorded in configuration with its source.
5. **Offline is the normal case.** Cloud connectivity is a bonus, not a dependency.
6. **Local safety first.** Emergency alerts must work with no internet.
7. **Measure, don't assume.** Power generation comes from voltage and current sensors, never inferred from the weather.
8. **Least privilege.** Engineers can view and acknowledge. They can't rewrite history.
9. **Configurable, not hard-coded.** Every electrical rating, interval and threshold lives in configuration.
10. **Test before you claim.** A capability is "supported" only when a documented test passes.

### 3.2 Claims policy

| ❌ Never write | ✅ Write instead |
|---|---|
| "Detects arsenic / heavy metals" | "Monitors water-quality parameters; contaminant screening requires a dedicated detection method" |
| "Water is SAFE" | "All configured parameters within configured bands; verification PASSED" |
| "IP67 waterproof" | "Designed for outdoor use with gasketed enclosure; ingress rating **not tested**" |
| "Military-grade security" | "Reasonable security practices: authentication, RBAC, TLS where supported, audit logging" |
| "Works in all weather" | "Designed to improve energy availability under variable weather; operation depends on measured generation and battery reserve" |
| "Industrial certified" | "Prototype; certification path described in §47" |
| "Battery lasts 30 days" | "Autonomy = usable capacity ÷ average load; both `TBD / CONFIGURABLE`, measured during Phase 13" |
| "Toxic level: TDS > X" | "ABN-001 attention band for TDS: `TBD / CONFIGURABLE`, source: ________" |

### 3.3 Terminology used in this document

| Term | Meaning in this project |
|---|---|
| **Water-quality parameter monitoring** | Continuous measurement of pH, turbidity, TDS, EC and temperature |
| **Verification** | Applying documented data-quality rules to a raw reading and recording the outcome |
| **Screening** | A dedicated, separate method aimed at a specific contaminant (not part of the core sensor set) |
| **Attention band** | Project-configured range outside which a reading is flagged `ABNORMAL` for human follow-up. It is not a safety verdict |
| **Sensor operating range** | The range the sensor hardware can physically report (from its datasheet). Values outside it suggest a sensor fault |
| **Prototype** | Built and tested at bench or pilot scale; not certified |
| **Industrial deployment capability** | A future target that needs further engineering, testing and certification |
| **`TBD / CONFIGURABLE`** | A value not decided yet; must be set in configuration before deployment |

## 4. System Architecture

### 4.1 Architecture diagram

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/01_system_architecture.png" alt="System architecture" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 4.1: Generated by <code>scripts/generate_diagrams.py → fig_system_architecture()</code>.</sub>

### 4.2 Layered view

```text
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ L6  PRESENTATION   Engineer Dashboard (simple)  │  Industrial Debugger (deep) │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ L5  CLOUD          Firebase Auth · Firestore · Security Rules · Functions     │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ L4  SYNC           Outbox queue · idempotent upsert · exponential backoff     │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ L3  EDGE TRUST     Receiver · Verification Engine · Append-only store · Audit │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ L2  FIELD NODE     ESP32: acquisition · calibration · modes · health · alerts │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ L1  SENSING        pH · Turbidity · TDS · EC · Temperature  (+ optional       │
│                    dedicated screening subsystem, separate)                   │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ L0  ENERGY         Solar + Wind → controllers → Battery → protected load bus  │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 4.3 Responsibility matrix

| Function | ESP32 node | Raspberry Pi edge | Firebase | Dashboard | Debugger |
|---|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
| Read sensors | **R** | | | | |
| Apply calibration coefficients | **R** | V | | | V |
| Generate record ID & sequence | **R** | V | | | |
| Local buzzer/LED alert | **R** | C | | | |
| Timestamp on receipt | | **R** | | | |
| Schema / format validation | basic | **R** | rules | | |
| Verification engine | | **R** | | view | view |
| Raw storage (authoritative) | ring buffer | **R** | replica | | |
| Offline queue | ring buffer | **R** | | | view |
| Cloud persistence | | | **R** | | |
| Access control | | local | **R** | enforced | enforced |
| Power telemetry | **R** (measure) | aggregate | store | view | view |
| Mode decision | **R** | advise | | view | view |
| Audit log | | **R** (local) | **R** (cloud) | view | view |

*R = responsible, V = verifies, C = contributes.*

### 4.4 Why both an ESP32 and a Raspberry Pi?

| Aspect | ESP32 | Raspberry Pi |
|---|---|---|
| Role | Real-time field I/O, deep sleep, deterministic sampling | Trusted processing, storage, sync, diagnostics |
| Power profile | Very low in deep sleep (measure on your board) | Much higher; can't practically deep-sleep like an MCU |
| Storage | Small flash; ring buffer only | SD/SSD; database-scale storage |
| OS | Bare-metal / FreeRTOS | Linux (systemd services) |
| Failure isolation | Keeps sensing and local alerting if the Pi is down | Keeps storing and verifying if the cloud is down |
| Update frequency | Rare (OTA with care) | More frequent (packages, services) |

The split means that a failure at any single layer doesn't stop sensing:

```text
Cloud down   → Pi stores + queues          → no data loss
Pi down      → ESP32 ring-buffers + alerts → bounded data loss protection
Modem down   → Pi stores + queues          → no data loss
Grid absent  → hybrid renewables + battery → designed-for condition
Battery low  → LOW-POWER mode, load shedding (P3/P4 first)
```

### 4.5 Deployment topologies

| Topology | Description | When to use |
|---|---|---|
| **T1: Co-located** | ESP32 and Pi in the same enclosure, UART link | Single-site pilot (default prototype) |
| **T2: Star** | Several ESP32 nodes → one Pi gateway over RS-485 or Wi-Fi | Several sampling points within tens to hundreds of metres |
| **T3: Node-direct** | ESP32 + cellular modem → Firebase; no Pi | Very low-power sites; loses edge verification (the cloud function runs the same engine) |
| **T4: Hybrid** | T1/T2 with cellular fallback directly from the ESP32 | High-value sites where the Pi is a single point of failure |

> **Default for this project: T1, with T4's fallback path optional.**

### 4.6 Interfaces summary

| Interface | From → To | Physical | Protocol | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IF-01 | Sensors → ESP32 | Analog / 1-Wire / I²C (sensor dependent) | Electrical | `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` per sensor |
| IF-02 | ESP32 → Pi | UART (3.3 V TTL) or RS-485 transceiver | Framed JSON + CRC-16 | Prototype: UART |
| IF-03 | ESP32 → Modem | UART | AT commands | Optional |
| IF-04 | Pi → Firebase | Ethernet/Wi-Fi/cellular router or USB modem | HTTPS (TLS) via Firebase Admin SDK | Prototype |
| IF-05 | Power monitor → ESP32 | I²C | Register reads | `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` |
| IF-06 | ESP32 → Buzzer/LEDs | GPIO (via transistor driver) | Digital | Prototype |
| IF-07 | Dashboard → Firebase | Internet | HTTPS / WebSocket (Firestore SDK) | Prototype |
| IF-08 | Debugger → Pi | LAN / local Wi-Fi AP | HTTP (local API, token auth) | Prototype |

## 5. Data Flow

### 5.1 End-to-end pipeline

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/03_data_pipeline.png" alt="Data pipeline" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 5.1: Raw data pipeline. The raw row is committed before verification runs.</sub>

### 5.2 Step-by-step

| Step | Where | Action | Output | Failure handling |
|:-:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ESP32 | Read each sensor N times, median filter (N configurable) | Raw electrical/converted values | Missing sensor → field set to `null`, never a fake value |
| 2 | ESP32 | Apply stored calibration coefficients (version-tagged) | Engineering-unit values + `cal_version` | Missing calibration → `cal_state: "UNCALIBRATED"` flag |
| 3 | ESP32 | Build frame: IDs, device timestamp, `boot_id`, `seq`, mode, power, comms | Raw JSON | Buffered in RTC/flash ring buffer |
| 4 | ESP32 → Pi | Send framed JSON with CRC-16 | Bytes on the wire | No ACK → retry, then keep in ring buffer |
| 5 | Pi | Check CRC, parse JSON, stamp `received_ts` | Parsed message | Bad CRC → `frame_error` event, NACK |
| 6 | Pi | **INSERT raw row** (append-only) + `raw_sha256` + chain hash | `raw_readings` row | DB error → local file spool (last resort) |
| 7 | Pi | Run verification engine | `verification_results` row | Engine exception → `ENGINE_ERROR` event; raw is already safe |
| 8 | Pi | Raise events/alerts from results | `events` rows, buzzer command | Local alert works offline |
| 9 | Pi | Enqueue raw + result + events into `sync_outbox` | `PENDING` rows | — |
| 10 | Pi → Cloud | Sync engine batches, upserts by deterministic ID | Firestore docs | Backoff; stays `PENDING` |
| 11 | Cloud | Security Rules reject updates/deletes on raw and audit | Immutable replica | Rule violation → denied + logged |
| 12 | UI | Dashboard subscribes; debugger queries edge API | Views | Stale-data banner if last sync is too old |

### 5.3 Raw frame (wire format, schema v1)

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "record_id": "SWN-0001-00017-00004521",
  "node_id": "SWN-0001",
  "boot_id": 17,
  "seq": 4521,
  "sensor_ids": {
    "ph": "PH-01", "turbidity_ntu": "TU-01", "tds_ppm": "TD-01",
    "ec_us_cm": "EC-01", "temperature_c": "TP-01"
  },
  "timestamp": "2026-09-23T05:12:05Z",
  "time_source": "RTC",
  "mode": "NORMAL",
  "raw": {
    "ph": 7.214,
    "turbidity_ntu": 4.81,
    "tds_ppm": 212.4,
    "ec_us_cm": 426.1,
    "temperature_c": 26.42
  },
  "raw_electrical": {
    "ph_mv": 1412.0, "turbidity_mv": 3021.0, "tds_mv": 611.0, "ec_mv": 702.0
  },
  "cal_version": { "ph": "2026-09-01T10:00Z#3", "ec": "2026-09-01T10:20Z#2" },
  "power": {
    "battery_v": 12.84, "battery_state": "CHARGING", "source": "SOLAR",
    "solar_w": 7.3, "wind_w": 0.0, "load_w": 3.4
  },
  "comm": { "link": "UART", "modem": "REGISTERED", "rssi_csq": 17, "retries": 0 },
  "fw": "0.4.2+a1b2c3"
}
```

> **Why `raw_electrical`?** Keeping the pre-calibration electrical value (millivolts) alongside the engineering value means a later calibration correction can be *re-computed* from the original signal, and filed as an audit event, without touching the raw record.

### 5.4 Identifier strategy

| Identifier | Format | Generated by | Uniqueness guarantee |
|---|---|---|---|
| `node_id` | `SWN-NNNN` | Provisioning (admin) | Registry in Firebase `nodes/` |
| `sensor_id` | `<TYPE>-<NN>` + serial in registry | Provisioning | Registry per node |
| `boot_id` | uint, incremented and persisted in NVS on every boot | ESP32 | Monotonic per node |
| `seq` | uint32, reset on boot | ESP32 | Monotonic within a boot |
| `record_id` | `<node_id>-<boot_id:05d>-<seq:08d>` | ESP32 | Globally unique (node × boot × seq) |
| `result_id` | `<record_id>#v<engine_version>` | Pi | One result per record per engine version |
| `event_id` | UUIDv7 (time-ordered) | Pi | Random + time |
| `audit_id` | UUIDv7 | Pi / Cloud Function | Random + time |

Because the `record_id` is deterministic, **the same reading always maps to the same Firestore document**. A re-sent frame after a lost ACK, or a re-synced batch after a timeout, overwrites nothing and creates no duplicate. See [§15](#15-offline-first-operation).

### 5.5 Data retention (defaults, configurable)

| Store | Retention | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| ESP32 ring buffer | Last N frames (N = `TBD / CONFIGURABLE`, flash-limited) | Survive a Pi outage |
| Pi `raw_readings` | Indefinite (rotate to archive files when storage > threshold) | Authoritative local record |
| Pi `sync_outbox` | Until ACKED + 7 days | Debugging sync issues |
| Pi log files | 30 days, rotated | Troubleshooting |
| Firestore `readings` | Indefinite (or export to cold storage) | Cloud history |
| Firestore `audit` | Indefinite | Traceability |

## 6. Power Flow & Hybrid Renewable Architecture

### 6.1 Architecture

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/02_power_architecture.png" alt="Hybrid power architecture" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 6.1: Hybrid renewable power architecture. All ratings are <code>TBD / CONFIGURABLE</code>.</sub>

```text
SOLAR PANEL  ──►  SOLAR CHARGE CONTROLLER (PWM/MPPT)       ──┐
                                                             ├──►  BATTERY  ──►  PROTECTION  ──►  LOAD-PRIORITY BUS
WIND TURBINE ──►  RECTIFIER + WIND CHARGE CONTROLLER       ──┘                   (fuse, TVS,      P1 ESP32 + sensors
                  (+ dump load / brake for over-speed)                          reverse-pol.,     P1 Local alert
                                                                                 LVD)              P2 Raspberry Pi
                          POWER MONITOR (V/I per source, battery, load)                            P3 Cellular modem
                                                                                                   P4 Purification aux.
```

### 6.2 Design rules

1. **One controller per source.** Solar and wind have very different electrical behaviour. PV is a current-limited DC source; a small turbine gives variable-frequency AC (or wild DC) and must never be left open-circuit at high wind speed. Never connect a turbine to a solar-only controller.
2. **Common battery.** Both controllers charge the same battery. The controllers, battery chemistry and charge profiles must be compatible (for example the same absorption/float set-points). `TBD / CONFIGURABLE`.
3. **Wind over-speed protection.** The wind controller must provide a dump (diversion) load and/or electrical braking, and the turbine should support mechanical furling where available.
4. **Measured, not assumed.** Each source has its own voltage/current measurement. The system never *infers* generation from the weather.
5. **Low-voltage disconnect (LVD).** A hardware LVD protects the battery from deep discharge, independently of the firmware.
6. **Protection on every branch.** Fuses sized for the conductor, TVS diodes on long cable runs, reverse-polarity protection, and a common earth/bonding point.

### 6.3 Source availability scenarios

| # | Solar | Wind | Battery role | System behaviour |
|:-:|:-:|:-:|---|---|
| 1 | ✅ available | ❌ unavailable | Charges from solar surplus; supplies at night | NORMAL if SoC is healthy |
| 2 | ❌ unavailable | ✅ available | Charges from wind surplus | NORMAL if wind covers the load |
| 3 | ✅ available | ✅ available | Charges fastest | NORMAL; purification aux (P4) may be enabled |
| 4 | ❌ unavailable | ❌ unavailable | **Sole supply**: BATTERY → LOAD | NORMAL until `LOW_ENTER`, then LOW-POWER; CRITICAL sheds P2–P4 |

> **No source is assumed to always be available.** The four scenarios are tested explicitly (see [Power tests PW-01…PW-09](#466-power-tests)).

### 6.4 Illustrative 4-day behaviour (simulated)

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/11_hybrid_power_4day.png" alt="Hybrid power simulation" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 6.2: SIMULATED four-day weather sequence (sunny → cloudy/windy → rain → storm), with an illustrative battery-state estimate and LOW-POWER mode activation. Units are normalised; thresholds are examples only.</sub>

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/12_power_source_timeline.png" alt="Power source timeline" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 6.3: SIMULATED active power source over time, and share of time per source.</sub>

**Reading these figures:**

- Day 1 (sunny, low wind): solar charges the battery during daylight; the battery carries the night.
- Day 2 (cloudy, windy): wind makes up for reduced solar and the battery stays healthy.
- Day 3 (rain, intermittent wind): generation often falls below load, so the battery discharges. If SoC falls below `LOW_ENTER`, LOW-POWER mode reduces load.
- Day 4 (storm): gusty wind contributes, but over-speed protection can brake the turbine, so wind generation drops to zero during the strongest gusts. This is why weather alone can't predict generation.

### 6.5 Energy budget template

> Fill this in with **measured** values from Phase 13. Do not use datasheet "typical" figures for deployment decisions.

| Load | Priority | Active current (A) | Active time per hour (s) | Sleep current (A) | Avg power (W) |
|---|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
| ESP32 + sensor excitation | P1 | TBD | TBD | TBD | TBD |
| Buzzer / LEDs (alert only) | P1 | TBD | event | 0 | TBD |
| Raspberry Pi (idle / active) | P2 | TBD | 3600 | n/a | TBD |
| Cellular modem (idle / TX burst) | P3 | TBD | TBD | TBD | TBD |
| Purification auxiliary | P4 | TBD | TBD | 0 | TBD |
| Controller + monitor self-consumption | — | TBD | 3600 | — | TBD |
| **Total average load** | | | | | **TBD** |

```text
Daily energy need (Wh/day)   E_load  = P_avg_total × 24
Usable battery energy (Wh)   E_usable = V_nominal × C_Ah × DoD_allowed × η_discharge
Autonomy (days, no input)    A = E_usable / E_load
Required daily harvest       E_harvest ≥ E_load / (η_charge × η_controller)
```

All variables are `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` and must be measured or taken from the purchased components' datasheets.

## 7. Communication Architecture

### 7.1 Paths

```text
PRIMARY (local, always)           CLOUD (when available)              FALLBACK (optional, T4)
ESP32                              Raspberry Pi                         ESP32
  │ UART / RS-485                    │ HTTPS/TLS                           │ UART (AT)
  ▼                                  ▼                                     ▼
Raspberry Pi                       Router / USB modem                   Cellular module (e.g. SIM800L)
  │                                  │                                     │ GPRS/2G (region-dependent)
  ▼                                  ▼                                     ▼
Local storage (SQLite)             Firebase                             Relay endpoint / Firebase
```

> **Cellular note.** SIM800L-class modules are 2G/GPRS devices. **2G networks are being shut down in several countries and regions**, so check local operator support before choosing a module. A 4G LTE Cat-M1/NB-IoT/Cat-1 module is the likely upgrade path and is kept behind the same `CommLink` interface. SIM800L-class modules also draw **high peak current during transmit bursts** and need a supply that can deliver it, with bulk capacitance close to the module (value `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` from the module datasheet).

### 7.2 Local link framing (ESP32 → Pi)

```text
┌──────┬──────┬────────┬───────────────────────────┬────────┬──────┐
│ 0x02 │ VER  │ LEN(2) │ JSON payload (UTF-8)       │ CRC16  │ 0x03 │
│ STX  │ 0x01 │ BE u16 │ ≤ 1024 bytes (configurable)│ CCITT  │ ETX  │
└──────┴──────┴────────┴───────────────────────────┴────────┴──────┘
Pi replies:  ACK <record_id>\n   or   NACK <reason>\n
```

- CRC-16/CCITT-FALSE over `VER | LEN | payload`.
- The ESP32 retries up to `link.max_retries` times (default 3) with `link.retry_backoff_ms`.
- Frames that are never ACKed stay in the ESP32 ring buffer and are replayed, oldest first, after the link recovers.
- The Pi treats a replayed `record_id` that it already stored as **ACK without re-insert** (idempotent).

### 7.3 Cloud link

- Firebase Admin SDK (Python) on the Pi, authenticated with a **service account whose key file never enters Git** (loaded from the `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` path or a secret store).
- Batched writes (≤ 500 operations per batch, per Firestore limits). Default batch size: 100.
- Document ID = `record_id`, which makes writes idempotent.
- Exponential backoff: `delay = min(max_delay, base × 2^attempt) + jitter`.

### 7.4 Communication status model

| State | Meaning | Detected by | Dashboard display |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LINK_OK` | ESP32 ↔ Pi frames ACKed | Pi receiver | Node ONLINE |
| `LINK_DEGRADED` | CRC errors or retries above threshold | Pi receiver counters | Node ONLINE (warning) |
| `LINK_DOWN` | No frame for > `heartbeat_timeout` | Pi watchdog | Node OFFLINE |
| `CLOUD_OK` | Last sync < `sync_stale_after` | Sync engine | Synced |
| `CLOUD_QUEUED` | Records pending, network down | Sync engine | "N records queued" |
| `CLOUD_AUTH_ERROR` | Credentials rejected | Sync engine | Debugger only + admin alert |
| `MODEM_NOT_REGISTERED` | AT+CREG not registered | ESP32 / Pi | Debugger: cellular panel |

## 8. Hardware Architecture

> **All electrical specifications below are `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` unless stated otherwise.** Part families are given as *examples* to aid prototyping; they aren't endorsements or verified selections.

### 8.1 Bill of materials (functional)

| Subsystem | Item | Example / family | Qty | Specification | Notes |
|---|---|---|:-:|---|---|
| Processing | ESP32 dev board or module | ESP32-WROOM-32 class | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Prefer modules with external antenna option if Wi-Fi is used |
| Sensing | pH probe + interface board | Analog pH kit (glass electrode) | 1 | Range per datasheet | Needs isolation from other probes (see 8.4) |
| Sensing | Turbidity sensor | Optical (IR scatter/transmission) module | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Many low-cost modules are *not* nephelometric; treat readings as relative unless calibrated against formazin standards |
| Sensing | TDS sensor | Analog TDS probe + board | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | TDS is usually *derived* from conductivity |
| Sensing | EC sensor | Conductivity probe (K = TBD) + board | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Temperature compensation required |
| Sensing | Temperature sensor | Waterproof DS18B20 class | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | 1-Wire, needs pull-up |
| Signal integrity | Isolated power / signal for analog probes | Isolated DC-DC + isolated ADC or isolator | per probe | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Prevents probe cross-talk through the water |
| Signal integrity | External ADC (optional) | 16-bit I²C ADC class | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | The ESP32's internal ADC is non-linear; external ADC recommended |
| Timekeeping | RTC with battery | DS3231 class | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Keeps time without network |
| Communication | Cellular module | SIM800L (2G) or LTE Cat-M1/NB-IoT module | 1 | Peak current per datasheet | Check network availability |
| Communication | External antenna + surge arrestor | Band-appropriate | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Mount outside enclosure |
| Edge | Raspberry Pi | Pi 4 / Pi 5 / CM4 class | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Industrial-temperature SBCs exist for later stages |
| Edge storage | High-endurance microSD or USB SSD | — | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | SSD preferred for write endurance |
| Power | Solar PV panel | Mono/poly | 1+ | W, Voc, Isc: TBD | |
| Power | Solar charge controller | PWM or MPPT | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Must match battery chemistry |
| Power | Small wind turbine | 3-blade micro turbine | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | With furling/brake |
| Power | Wind charge controller / rectifier + dump load | — | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Never leave turbine unloaded |
| Power | Rechargeable battery | Chemistry TBD (e.g. LiFePO₄ or sealed lead-acid) | 1 | Capacity: TBD | **Capacity is not specified in this document** |
| Power | Battery management / protection | BMS (for lithium), LVD | 1 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | |
| Power | Power monitors | INA219/INA226/INA3221 class (I²C) | 3–4 | Shunt values TBD | Solar, wind, battery, load |
| Power | DC-DC converters | Buck (12 V→5 V, 5 V→3.3 V) | 2+ | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Pi needs a stable, adequately rated 5 V |
| Power | Load switches | High-side MOSFET switches | 3 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | For P2, P3, P4 shedding |
| Protection | Fuses / holders, TVS diodes, reverse-polarity MOSFET | — | set | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | |
| Alerts | Buzzer (active), LEDs (G/Y/R) + drivers | — | 1 + 3 | TBD / CONFIGURABLE | Panel-mount, sealed if possible |

| Mounting | Pole, brackets, sensor guard pipe | Galvanised steel / PVC | set | TBD | |

### 8.2 ESP32 pin allocation (default, configurable)

> These defaults live in `smart-water-esp32/config/pins.h` and can be changed. They avoid ESP32 strapping pins and keep analog inputs on **ADC1**, because ADC2 can't be used while Wi-Fi is active.

| Function | Default GPIO | Interface | Notes |
|---|:-:|---|---|
| pH analog | 36 (VP) | ADC1_CH0 | Input-only pin |
| Turbidity analog | 39 (VN) | ADC1_CH3 | Input-only pin |
| TDS analog | 34 | ADC1_CH6 | Input-only pin |
| EC analog | 35 | ADC1_CH7 | Input-only pin |
| Temperature (1-Wire) | 4 | 1-Wire | 4.7 kΩ pull-up (typical, verify) |
| I²C SDA | 21 | I²C | Power monitors, RTC, external ADC |
| I²C SCL | 22 | I²C | |
| UART2 TX → Pi RX | 17 | UART2 | 3.3 V logic |
| UART2 RX ← Pi TX | 16 | UART2 | |
| Modem TX/RX | 25 / 26 | UART1 (remapped) | Optional |
| Modem reset | 27 | GPIO | Optional |
| Buzzer | 13 | GPIO → transistor | |
| LED green / yellow / red | 32 / 33 / 14 | GPIO | |
| Load switch P2 (Pi) | 18 | GPIO | Default ON |
| Load switch P3 (modem) | 19 | GPIO | |
| Load switch P4 (aux) | 23 | GPIO | Default OFF |
| Sensor excitation enable | 5 | GPIO | Power sensors only while sampling (strapping pin: must be HIGH at boot; verify board) |

### 8.3 Block wiring (logical)

```text
                  ┌─────────────────────────── 12 V (nominal, TBD) DC BUS ─────────────────────────────┐
                  │                                                                                    │
     [BATTERY]──[FUSE]──[REV-POL]──[LVD]──┬──[BUCK 5V]──[LOAD SW P2]──► Raspberry Pi                    │
                                          ├──[BUCK 5V/4V]──[LOAD SW P3]──► Cellular modem              │
                                          ├──[BUCK 3V3]──► ESP32 ──► [EXC EN]──► sensor boards         │
                                          └──[LOAD SW P4]──► Purification auxiliary                    │
                                                                                                       │
 [INA #1 solar]──I²C─┐   [INA #2 wind]──I²C─┐   [INA #3 battery]──I²C─┐   [INA #4 load]──I²C─┐         │
                     └──────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──► ESP32 I²C
```

### 8.4 Analog sensor interference

Several low-cost pH, EC and TDS modules share a common ground through the water. If they're powered at the same time from the same supply, the probes can interfere with each other and give wrong readings. Mitigations, in order of preference:

1. **Galvanic isolation** of each analog probe (isolated DC-DC plus isolated signal path).
2. **Time-multiplexed excitation**: power one conductivity-type probe at a time, and let it settle for `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` ms before reading.
3. **Physical separation** of the probes in the flow cell.

This is verified by test **SN-06** (cross-interference): each probe's reading must stay within tolerance when the other probes are switched on or off.

### 8.5 Hardware configuration file (example)

```yaml
# smart-water-esp32/config/hardware.example.yaml  (copied to hardware.yaml, not secret)
node:
  id: SWN-0001                 # assigned at provisioning
  topology: T1
sensors:
  ph:          { id: PH-01, interface: adc1, pin: 36, samples: 15, settle_ms: TBD }
  turbidity:   { id: TU-01, interface: adc1, pin: 39, samples: 15, settle_ms: TBD }
  tds:         { id: TD-01, interface: adc1, pin: 34, samples: 15, settle_ms: TBD, excitation_group: B }
  ec:          { id: EC-01, interface: adc1, pin: 35, samples: 15, settle_ms: TBD, excitation_group: C, cell_k: TBD }
  temperature: { id: TP-01, interface: onewire, pin: 4 }
adc:
  type: internal               # internal | ads1115 | ...
  attenuation_db: 11
power_monitor:
  solar:   { type: TBD, i2c_addr: TBD, shunt_ohm: TBD }
  wind:    { type: TBD, i2c_addr: TBD, shunt_ohm: TBD }
  battery: { type: TBD, i2c_addr: TBD, shunt_ohm: TBD }
  load:    { type: TBD, i2c_addr: TBD, shunt_ohm: TBD }
battery:
  chemistry: TBD               # LiFePO4 | SLA | ...
  nominal_v: TBD
  capacity_ah: TBD             # NOT specified by this project
  soc_method: voltage_lut      # voltage_lut | coulomb_count | bms
link:
  pi:    { type: uart, baud: 115200, max_retries: 3, retry_backoff_ms: 250 }
  modem: { enabled: false, type: sim800l, apn_env: MODEM_APN }
```

## 9. Industrial & Environmental Design

> **No IP rating, certification or "military-grade" claim is made.** The measures below are *design intent*. Their effectiveness must be verified through the tests in [§46.9](#469-environmental--enclosure-tests-future--pilot) before anyone relies on them.

### 9.1 Enclosure concept

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/22_enclosure_concept.png" alt="Enclosure concept" width="90%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 9.1: Layout reference generated in Python. Not a mechanical drawing.</sub>

### 9.2 Environmental threat → design response

| Threat | Effect on system | Design response | Verification (planned) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dust** | Connector contamination, fan/vent clogging, optical sensor fouling | Sealed enclosure, gasketed lid, no fans (passive cooling), dust caps on unused connectors | Visual inspection after pilot period; dust exposure test (future) |
| **Rain** | Water ingress, short circuits, corrosion | Cable glands on the bottom face only, drip loops, sloped rain hood, sealed connectors | Hose/spray test (informal, not a rated test) |
| **Humidity / condensation** | Corrosion, leakage currents, ADC drift | Breather/pressure-equalisation vent, desiccant pack, conformal coating on PCBs (optional) | Humidity logging inside enclosure (internal RH sensor, optional) |
| **Temperature variation** | Component derating, battery performance change, sensor drift | Sun shield, light-coloured enclosure, battery in a separate compartment, temperature compensation for EC/TDS | Internal temperature logging |
| **Solar UV** | Plastic embrittlement, cable jacket degradation | UV-stable enclosure material, UV-rated cable, conduit | Periodic inspection |
| **Electrical noise** | ADC noise, false readings, resets | Shielded sensor cables, star grounding, ferrites, filtering, median sampling, isolated analog front-ends | Noise-floor measurement in SN tests |
| **Lightning / surge** | Component destruction | Surge arrestor on antenna, TVS on long lines, earthing rod and bonding | Design review (surge testing is future work) |
| **Long-duration operation** | SD-card wear, memory leaks, clock drift | SSD or high-endurance SD, WAL mode, watchdogs on ESP32 and Pi, RTC with NTP/GSM sync | 7-day and 30-day soak tests |
| **Remote location** | Slow maintenance response | Self-diagnostics, remote debugger, low-power fallback | Integration tests |
| **Limited maintenance** | Sensor fouling, drift | Guard pipe, anti-fouling approach (TBD), calibration reminders | Maintenance schedule (§44) |
| **Theft / vandalism** | Loss of equipment | Tamper-resistant fasteners, lockable enclosure, height mounting, optional door switch | Door-open event test |
| **Animals / insects** | Chewed cables, nests | Conduit, sealed glands, mesh on vents | Inspection |
| **Wind loading** | Mast/turbine damage | Guyed mast, turbine furling/brake | Structural review (future) |

### 9.3 Cable & connector practice

- Use **waterproof circular connectors** (where applicable) for sensors, so a probe can be swapped without opening the main enclosure.
- **Cable glands** sized to the cable outer diameter; unused glands blanked.
- **Drip loops** below every entry point.
- Sensor cables run in **flexible conduit** to the guard pipe.
- **Label** every cable at both ends with a sensor ID that matches the registry (`PH-01` and so on).
- Keep **power and analog signal cables separate**, and cross them at 90° where they must cross.

### 9.4 Sensor protection

| Measure | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Perforated guard pipe / flow cell | Physical protection, reduced debris, stable immersion depth |
| Probe holders with depth stop | Repeatable positioning after maintenance |
| Wet storage caps for pH probe during transport | Glass electrodes must not dry out |
| Anti-fouling strategy (wiper, shade, or cleaning schedule) | Turbidity and EC surfaces foul over time: `TBD / CONFIGURABLE` |
| Quick-release mounts | Faster maintenance |

### 9.5 Serviceability

- All field-replaceable units (FRUs) are reachable without removing the enclosure from the pole.
- Each FRU has a **debugger procedure** (see [§19](#19-industrial-debugger)) to verify it after replacement.
- Maintenance actions are **logged as events** (who, when, what), and are never used to alter raw data.

| FRU | Replace without tools? | Post-replacement check |
|---|:-:|---|
| pH probe | ✅ (connector) | Calibration + DIAG-SN-PH |
| Turbidity / EC / TDS probe | ✅ (connector) | Calibration + DIAG-SN-xx |
| Temperature probe | ✅ | Comparison against reference thermometer |
| ESP32 board | ❌ (screwdriver) | Provision node ID, firmware check, full diagnostic |
| Raspberry Pi / SSD | ❌ | Restore config, DB integrity check, sync test |
| Modem / SIM | ❌ | Registration + signal check |
| Battery | ❌ | Voltage, polarity, charge test |

### 9.6 Dedicated contaminant screening subsystem (optional, separate)

The core sensors measure **general water-quality parameters**. If a project needs screening for a specific contaminant (for example arsenic in some groundwater regions, or heavy metals near mining sites), treat it as a **separate subsystem**:

```text
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DEDICATED SCREENING SUBSYSTEM (optional, NOT part of the prototype)          │
│                                                                              │
│  Method examples (to be selected with domain experts):                       │
│   • Field test kits (manual, result entered as a SCREENING event)            │
│   • Automated colorimetric / electrochemical analyser (vendor-specific)      │
│   • Scheduled sample collection → accredited laboratory analysis             │
│                                                                              │
│  Integration:                                                                │
│   • Results stored in a SEPARATE `screening_results` collection              │
│   • Each result records: method, analyte, value, unit, LOD, operator/device, │
│     sample ID, chain-of-custody reference                                    │
│   • Core verification engine NEVER infers analyte presence from pH/TDS/EC    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Core-parameter changes, such as a sudden pH drop or a sharp EC rise, may **prompt** screening under a project procedure. They are never reported as detection of a contaminant.

---

# PART B: DATA, VERIFICATION AND TRUST

## 10. Raw Data Model: Preserve Everything

### 10.1 Principle

> The system **never** stores only `SAFE / UNSAFE`. It stores the **original reading** and, separately, the **verification result** that refers to it.

### 10.2 Mandatory raw fields

| Field | Type | Required | Source | Example |
|---|---|:-:|---|---|
| `record_id` | string | ✅ | ESP32 | `SWN-0001-00017-00004521` |
| `node_id` | string | ✅ | ESP32 (provisioned) | `SWN-0001` |
| `sensor_ids` | map | ✅ | ESP32 (provisioned) | `{"ph":"PH-01",...}` |
| `timestamp` (device) | ISO-8601 UTC | ✅ | ESP32 RTC | `2026-09-23T05:12:05Z` |
| `received_ts` | ISO-8601 UTC | ✅ | Pi | `2026-09-23T05:12:06.214Z` |
| `raw.ph` | float \| null | ✅ | pH sensor | `7.214` |
| `raw.turbidity_ntu` | float \| null | ✅ | Turbidity sensor | `4.81` |
| `raw.tds_ppm` | float \| null | ✅ | TDS sensor | `212.4` |
| `raw.ec_us_cm` | float \| null | ✅ | EC sensor | `426.1` |
| `raw.temperature_c` | float \| null | ✅ | Temperature sensor | `26.42` |
| `mode` | enum | ✅ | ESP32 | `NORMAL` \| `LOW_POWER` \| `EMERGENCY` |
| `power` | object | ✅ | ESP32 power monitor | battery V, source, W per source |
| `comm` | object | ✅ | ESP32 | link, retries, modem state |
| `verification_status` | enum | ✅ (in result table) | Pi engine | `VALID` … |
| `raw_electrical` | object | recommended | ESP32 | mV values before calibration |
| `cal_version` | map | recommended | ESP32 | calibration record IDs |
| `fw` | string | recommended | ESP32 | `0.4.2+a1b2c3` |
| `raw_sha256` | hex | ✅ | Pi | canonical-JSON SHA-256 |
| `chain_hash` | hex | ✅ | Pi | `H(prev_chain ‖ raw_sha256)` |

**`null` is a first-class value.** A disconnected sensor produces `null`, never `0`, never "the last value", and never an interpolated guess.

### 10.3 Data model overview

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/24_data_model.png" alt="Data model" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 10.1: Edge SQLite tables and their Firestore mirrors.</sub>

### 10.4 Edge SQLite schema (DDL)

```sql
-- smart-water-raspberry-pi/database/schema.sql
PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS nodes (
    node_id          TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    location_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
    deployment_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (deployment_state IN
                     ('PROVISIONED','COMMISSIONING','READY_FOR_DEPLOYMENT','DEPLOYED_VERIFIED','MAINTENANCE','RETIRED')),
    sensor_ids_json  TEXT NOT NULL,
    fw_version       TEXT,
    registered_ts    TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS raw_readings (
    record_id        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    node_id          TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES nodes(node_id),
    boot_id          INTEGER NOT NULL,
    seq              INTEGER NOT NULL,
    sensor_ids_json  TEXT NOT NULL,
    device_ts        TEXT NOT NULL,
    received_ts      TEXT NOT NULL,
    mode             TEXT NOT NULL,
    ph               REAL,
    turbidity_ntu    REAL,
    tds_ppm          REAL,
    ec_us_cm         REAL,
    temperature_c    REAL,
    raw_electrical_json TEXT,
    cal_version_json TEXT,
    power_json       TEXT NOT NULL,
    comm_json        TEXT NOT NULL,
    fw_version       TEXT,
    payload_json     TEXT NOT NULL,        -- full original message, verbatim
    raw_sha256       TEXT NOT NULL,
    prev_chain_hash  TEXT NOT NULL,
    chain_hash       TEXT NOT NULL,
    UNIQUE (node_id, boot_id, seq)
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ix_raw_node_ts ON raw_readings(node_id, device_ts);

-- Append-only enforcement at the database layer
CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS trg_raw_no_update
BEFORE UPDATE ON raw_readings
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'raw_readings is append-only'); END;

CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS trg_raw_no_delete
BEFORE DELETE ON raw_readings
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'raw_readings is append-only'); END;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS verification_results (
    result_id        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,           -- <record_id>#v<engine_version>
    record_id        TEXT NOT NULL,              -- no FK: INVALID frames may lack a raw row
    node_id          TEXT,
    status           TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN
                     ('VALID','INVALID','ABNORMAL','SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR','COMMUNICATION_ERROR')),
    verification     TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (verification IN ('VERIFICATION_PASSED','VERIFICATION_FAILED')),
    rules_json       TEXT NOT NULL,
    raw_snapshot_json TEXT NOT NULL,
    raw_sha256       TEXT NOT NULL,
    engine_version   TEXT NOT NULL,
    config_hash      TEXT NOT NULL,
    verified_ts      TEXT NOT NULL
);
CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS trg_vr_no_update BEFORE UPDATE ON verification_results
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'verification_results is append-only'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS trg_vr_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON verification_results
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'verification_results is append-only'); END;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS events (
    event_id     TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    node_id      TEXT,
    ts           TEXT NOT NULL,
    category     TEXT NOT NULL,     -- SENSOR | COMM | POWER | VERIFICATION | MAINTENANCE | SYSTEM | DEPLOYMENT
    type         TEXT NOT NULL,     -- e.g. SENSOR_DISCONNECTED, BATTERY_LOW
    severity     TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (severity IN ('INFO','WARNING','ALARM','CRITICAL')),
    is_alert     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    record_ref   TEXT,
    payload_json TEXT
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS alert_state_log (   -- alert lifecycle, append-only
    id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    event_id     TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES events(event_id),
    prev_state   TEXT,
    new_state    TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (new_state IN ('RAISED','LOCAL_ALERTED','NOTIFIED','ACKNOWLEDGED','RESOLVED','CLOSED')),
    actor        TEXT NOT NULL,
    note         TEXT,
    ts           TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS power_samples (
    sample_id    TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    node_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
    ts           TEXT NOT NULL,
    solar_v REAL, solar_i REAL, solar_p REAL,
    wind_v  REAL, wind_i  REAL, wind_p  REAL,
    battery_v REAL, battery_i REAL, soc_est REAL,
    load_p  REAL,
    source  TEXT NOT NULL,
    charge_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (charge_state IN ('CHARGING','DISCHARGING','IDLE','FAULT','UNKNOWN'))
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS audit_log (
    audit_id       TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    ts             TEXT NOT NULL,
    actor_uid      TEXT NOT NULL,
    actor_role     TEXT NOT NULL,
    action         TEXT NOT NULL,
    target_ref     TEXT NOT NULL,
    previous_value TEXT,
    new_value      TEXT,
    reason         TEXT NOT NULL,
    prev_hash      TEXT NOT NULL,
    hash           TEXT NOT NULL
);
CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS trg_audit_no_update BEFORE UPDATE ON audit_log
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'audit_log is append-only'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS trg_audit_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON audit_log
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'audit_log is append-only'); END;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sync_outbox (
    outbox_id        INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    collection       TEXT NOT NULL,    -- readings | results | alerts | power | audit | events
    doc_id           TEXT NOT NULL,
    payload_json     TEXT NOT NULL,
    state            TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PENDING' CHECK (state IN ('PENDING','SENT','ACKED','DEAD')),
    attempts         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    next_attempt_ts  TEXT NOT NULL,
    last_error       TEXT,
    created_ts       TEXT NOT NULL,
    UNIQUE (collection, doc_id)
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ix_outbox_state ON sync_outbox(state, next_attempt_ts);
```

> **Defence in depth.** SQLite triggers stop *accidental* or *application-level* modification. Someone with root access to the Pi could still alter the file, which is why the hash chain (§13.4) and cloud replication exist: tampering becomes **detectable**, even where it can't be made impossible.

### 10.5 Corrections: never edit, always append

If a reading is later found to be wrong, for example because the calibration was applied incorrectly:

```text
raw_readings[SWN-0001-00017-00004521]  ─── unchanged forever
         ▲
         │ target_ref
audit_log[01J8…]  action = "CORRECTION_ANNOTATION"
                  previous_value = {"ph": 7.214, "cal_version": "#3"}
                  new_value      = {"ph_corrected": 7.051, "cal_version": "#4"}
                  reason         = "pH buffer 7.00 was expired; recalibrated 2026-09-24, re-derived from raw_electrical.ph_mv"
                  actor_uid      = "admin:u_8f2…", actor_role = "ADMIN"
```

The dashboard shows the original value and an **"annotated"** badge linking to the audit record. It never silently swaps the number.

---

## 11. Raspberry Pi: Trusted Edge Layer

### 11.1 Responsibilities (15)

| # | Responsibility | Service / module | Output |
|:-:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Receive raw data from the ESP32 | `receiver/serial_receiver.py` | parsed frames |
| 2 | Timestamp incoming data | `receiver/` | `received_ts` |
| 3 | Validate message format | `validator/schema.py` | FMT-* hits |
| 4 | Check sensor identity | `validator/identity.py` | NID-* hits |
| 5 | Check expected data ranges | `validator/rules/range.py` | RNG-001 hits |
| 6 | Detect missing values | `validator/rules/missing.py` | MIS-001 hits |
| 7 | Detect impossible values | `validator/rules/format.py` | FMT-005 hits |
| 8 | Detect sudden abnormal changes | `validator/rules/rate.py` | ROC-001 hits |
| 9 | Run predefined formulas/algorithms | `algorithms/` | temperature compensation, TDS/EC consistency, rolling statistics |
| 10 | Store raw data locally | `storage/raw_store.py` | `raw_readings` |
| 11 | Store verification results | `storage/result_store.py` | `verification_results` |
| 12 | Store diagnostic events | `diagnostics/events.py` | `events` |
| 13 | Queue unsynchronised cloud data | `sync/outbox.py` | `sync_outbox` |
| 14 | Synchronise when connectivity returns | `sync/engine.py` | Firestore docs |
| 15 | Monitor system health | `diagnostics/health.py` | CPU, disk, temperature, service status |

### 11.2 What the Pi does **not** do

- It does **not** decide whether water is safe to drink.
- It does **not** "correct" readings.
- It does **not** infer contaminants from general parameters.
- It does **not** delete records to free space. Instead it archives them to compressed files, with hashes, and raises a storage alert.

### 11.3 Services (systemd)

| Unit | Purpose | Restart policy | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sw-receiver.service` | Serial receiver + storage + verification | `always`, 5 s | local FS |
| `sw-sync.service` | Outbox → Firebase | `always`, 10 s | network-online (soft) |
| `sw-health.service` | Health metrics, watchdog, disk checks | `always` | — |
| `sw-api.service` | Local read-only API for the debugger (LAN) | `always` | receiver DB |
| `sw-alert.service` | Local alert fan-out (GPIO buzzer on Pi, optional) | `always` | receiver |

```ini
# smart-water-raspberry-pi/deploy/systemd/sw-receiver.service
[Unit]
Description=Smart Water - serial receiver, raw store and verification engine
After=local-fs.target
StartLimitIntervalSec=0

[Service]
Type=simple
User=swedge
Group=swedge
WorkingDirectory=/opt/smart-water
EnvironmentFile=/etc/smart-water/edge.env
ExecStart=/opt/smart-water/.venv/bin/python -m swedge.receiver
Restart=always
RestartSec=5
WatchdogSec=60
NoNewPrivileges=true
ProtectSystem=strict
ReadWritePaths=/var/lib/smart-water /var/log/smart-water
PrivateTmp=true

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target
```

### 11.4 Edge configuration (example)

```yaml
# smart-water-raspberry-pi/config/edge.example.yaml
edge:
  gateway_id: GW-01
  db_path: /var/lib/smart-water/edge.db
  archive_dir: /var/lib/smart-water/archive
  storage_alert_percent: 80
receiver:
  port: /dev/serial0
  baud: 115200
  max_frame_bytes: 1024
  heartbeat_timeout_s: 900          # > 2× the longest configured sampling interval
verification:
  config_file: /etc/smart-water/verification.yaml
sync:
  enabled: true
  batch_size: 100
  base_delay_s: 2
  max_delay_s: 900
  jitter_s: 3
  stale_after_s: 1800
  dead_after_attempts: 0            # 0 = never mark DEAD automatically
health:
  interval_s: 60
  cpu_temp_warn_c: 70               # TBD / CONFIGURABLE
api:
  bind: 0.0.0.0
  port: 8088
  token_env: SW_DEBUG_API_TOKEN     # value lives in /etc/smart-water/edge.env (never in Git)
```

## 12. Data Verification Engine

### 12.1 Contract

```text
            ┌──────────────────────────────┐
RAW SENSOR  │                              │  VERIFICATION RESULT
DATA  ─────►│    VERIFICATION ENGINE       │──────────────────────►  status, verification,
(unchanged) │  (pure function + per-node   │                         rules_triggered[ {rule_id,
            │   short history)             │                         parameter, status, reason} ],
            └──────────────────────────────┘                         verified_at, raw_snapshot,
                                                                     raw_sha256, engine_version
```

**Output fields (required):** verification status · reason · rule triggered · timestamp · original raw value.
**The raw input object is never mutated.** Unit test `test_raw_value_not_modified` enforces this.

### 12.2 Decision flow

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/04_verification_flowchart.png" alt="Verification flowchart" width="70%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 12.1: Verification decision flow. Hard-fail rules stop early; soft rules all run so that every issue is reported together.</sub>

### 12.3 Classification outputs

| Status | Meaning | Typical cause | Verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| `VALID` | No rule triggered | Normal operation | `VERIFICATION_PASSED` |
| `ABNORMAL` | Data is structurally fine but behaves unusually (sudden change, outside a *configured* attention band) | Real water event **or** sensor event; human follow-up needed | `VERIFICATION_FAILED` |
| `SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR` | Value missing, outside sensor operating range, stuck, or inconsistent with related sensors | Disconnected/fouled/failed sensor | `VERIFICATION_FAILED` |
| `COMMUNICATION_ERROR` | Sequence gap: messages lost | Link noise, buffer overflow, reboot during send | `VERIFICATION_FAILED` |
| `INVALID` | Structurally unusable: malformed, duplicate, unregistered, bad timestamp | Firmware bug, spoofing attempt, clock fault | `VERIFICATION_FAILED` |

**Severity order** used to aggregate several hits into one overall status:
`VALID (0) < ABNORMAL (1) < SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR (2) < COMMUNICATION_ERROR (3) < INVALID (4)`

> The overall status is the most severe hit, but **all** hits are stored in `rules_triggered`, so no information is lost by aggregating.

### 12.4 Verification Rule Catalogue

> Every rule implemented in code must appear here with the same ID. Thresholds are **configuration**, not code.

| Rule ID | Family | Check | Triggers when | Result status | Hard-fail? | Config keys |
|---|---|---|---|---|:-:|---|
| **FMT-001** | Format | Message type | Payload isn't a JSON object | INVALID | ✅ | — |
| **FMT-002** | Format | Required fields | Any of `schema_version, record_id, node_id, sensor_ids, timestamp, seq, mode, raw, power, comm` missing | INVALID | ✅ | — |
| **FMT-003** | Format | Schema version | `schema_version` not supported | INVALID | | `supported_schema_versions` |
| **FMT-004** | Format | Raw block | `raw` isn't an object | INVALID | ✅ | — |
| **FMT-005** | Format | Numeric type | Parameter value is non-numeric, boolean or NaN | INVALID | | — |
| **DUP-001** | Duplicate | Record uniqueness | `record_id` already processed | INVALID | ✅ | — |
| **NID-001** | Node identity | Registered node | `node_id` not in registry (when a registry is configured) | INVALID | | `registered_nodes` |
| **NID-002** | Node identity | Registered sensors | A `sensor_id` isn't registered to this node | INVALID | | `registered_nodes[*].sensor_ids` |
| **TS-001** | Timestamp | Parse | Timestamp missing, malformed or without a timezone | INVALID | | — |
| **TS-002** | Timestamp | Future | Device timestamp > received time + `max_clock_skew_s` | INVALID | | `max_clock_skew_s` |
| **TS-003** | Timestamp | Stale | Device timestamp older than `max_age_s` | INVALID | | `max_age_s` |
| **TS-004** | Timestamp | Monotonic | Timestamp ≤ previous accepted timestamp for this node | INVALID | | — |
| **COM-001** | Communication | Sequence continuity | `seq > last_seq + 1` within the same boot | COMMUNICATION_ERROR | | — |
| **MIS-001** | Missing | Presence | Parameter value is `null`/absent | SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR | | — |
| **RNG-001** | Range | Sensor operating range | Value outside the **sensor's** configured operating range | SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR | | `ranges.<param>` |
| **ROC-001** | Rate of change | Plausible dynamics | `|Δvalue| / Δt(min) > max_rate_per_min.<param>` | ABNORMAL | | `max_rate_per_min.<param>` |
| **HLT-001** | Sensor health | Stuck value | Identical value for `stuck_value_window` consecutive samples | SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR | | `stuck_value_window` |
| **CON-001** | Consistency | TDS ↔ EC | `|TDS − k·EC| / (k·EC) > tds_ec_tolerance` | SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR | | `tds_ec_factor`, `tds_ec_tolerance` |
| **ABN-001** | Attention band | Project band | Value outside a **project-configured** attention band (disabled by default) | ABNORMAL | | `attention_bands.<param>` + `source` |

**Rule families: 11. Rule IDs: 19.** All are implemented in [`reference/edge/verification_engine.py`](reference/edge/verification_engine.py) and covered by [`reference/tests/test_verification_engine.py`](reference/tests/test_verification_engine.py).

### 12.5 Rule rationale

<details>
<summary><b>FMT: Format validation</b></summary>

*Is the message correctly structured?* Malformed frames can come from electrical noise that gets past the CRC (rare), from firmware bugs, or from deliberate injection. They're rejected as `INVALID`, and the **payload is still logged** in the event store for debugging. It isn't written into `raw_readings`, because it can't be trusted as a reading.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>RNG: Range validation</b></summary>

*Is the value within the configured sensor operating range?* This is **not** a water-safety check. A pH of 15.2 can't come from a working 0–14 pH sensor, so the likely explanation is a sensor or wiring fault. The ranges must come from the **datasheet of the sensor actually installed**.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>ROC: Rate-of-change validation</b></summary>

*Did the parameter change unrealistically quickly?* Water bodies have physical inertia. A jump of several pH units in 5 minutes is more likely a sensor event (air bubble, probe removed, cable fault) than a real change. It **could** also be a real event, such as a contamination slug. That's why ROC-001 is classified `ABNORMAL` (human follow-up) rather than `INVALID`. Rates are normalised per minute, so the rule works the same way in all three operating modes.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>HLT: Sensor health</b></summary>

*Is the sensor responding?* A disconnected analog input often floats or rails to a constant value, and a frozen driver repeats the last reading. Readings that are *exactly* identical over N samples are suspicious for noisy analog sensors. N must be tuned per sensor. A digital temperature sensor with 0.0625 °C resolution in a very stable tank may legitimately repeat, so tune `stuck_value_window` for each parameter where needed.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>CON: Consistency check</b></summary>

*Are related measurements behaving consistently according to predefined rules?* TDS meters usually derive TDS from conductivity using a conversion factor k (often in the range of roughly 0.5–0.7, depending on water composition; **`TBD / CONFIGURABLE`, determine for your site**). If the separate TDS and EC probes disagree beyond a tolerance, at least one of them is probably faulty or fouled.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>TS: Timestamp validation</b></summary>

*Is the timestamp valid?* Requires timezone-aware ISO-8601, a timestamp not in the future (beyond the skew allowance), not older than the maximum age, and monotonic per node. The ESP32 uses an RTC; the Pi's `received_ts` is always stored as an independent second clock.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>NID: Node ID validation</b></summary>

*Is the data associated with a registered node?* Nodes and their sensors are provisioned in a registry. Frames from unknown nodes or with swapped sensor IDs are rejected. This catches mis-wired replacements and basic spoofing.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>COM / DUP: Communication and duplicates</b></summary>

A gap in `seq` means frames were lost; the reading itself may be fine. A repeated `record_id` means a retransmission. It's rejected as a duplicate but **acknowledged**, so the ESP32 stops retrying.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>ABN: Attention bands (opt-in)</b></summary>

Disabled by default. When enabled, every band **must** carry a `source` field (for example a named standard, a regulator's guidance document, or a documented project decision with its date and approver). The engine refuses to load a band without a source (Phase 5 config validation).
</details>

### 12.6 Verification configuration (example)

```yaml
# /etc/smart-water/verification.yaml   (version-controlled as verification.example.yaml)
engine_version: 0.1.0
supported_schema_versions: [1]
ranges:                     # SENSOR OPERATING RANGES from datasheets — NOT safety limits
  ph:            [0.0, 14.0]
  turbidity_ntu: [0.0, TBD]
  tds_ppm:       [0.0, TBD]
  ec_us_cm:      [0.0, TBD]
  temperature_c: [TBD, TBD]
max_rate_per_min:           # TBD / CONFIGURABLE — tune from baseline data
  ph: 0.5
  turbidity_ntu: 200
  tds_ppm: 100
  ec_us_cm: 200
  temperature_c: 2.0
stuck_value_window: 6
tds_ec_factor: TBD          # determine per site
tds_ec_tolerance: 0.35
max_clock_skew_s: 300
max_age_s: 604800
attention_bands:            # DISABLED unless a verified source is supplied
  ph:            { enabled: false, low: null, high: null, source: null }
  turbidity_ntu: { enabled: false, low: null, high: null, source: null }
  tds_ppm:       { enabled: false, low: null, high: null, source: null }
  ec_us_cm:      { enabled: false, low: null, high: null, source: null }
  temperature_c: { enabled: false, low: null, high: null, source: null }
```

### 12.7 Engine behaviour on a simulated stream

The reference engine was run over a **simulated** 24-hour stream (288 samples at 5-minute intervals), with six injected faults:

| Injected at sample | Fault | Expected rule |
|:-:|---|---|
| 60 | pH jumps to 9.9 | ROC-001 |
| 120–123 | Turbidity sensor returns `null` | MIS-001 |
| 150 | TDS = 1450 (outside the 0–1000 placeholder range) | RNG-001 |
| 200 | 3 frames lost (seq jumps) | COM-001 |
| 230–237 | Temperature frozen at 25.00 | HLT-001 |
| 260 | TDS multiplied by 1.9 | CON-001 |

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/09_sensor_timeseries_flags.png" alt="Sensor time series with flags" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 12.2: SIMULATED raw stream. Markers show records where the engine raised a rule hit.</sub>

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/10_verification_distribution.png" alt="Verification distribution" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 12.3: SIMULATED status distribution and rule-hit counts. Every injected fault was detected by the expected rule (asserted in <code>test_simulated_stream_triggers_expected_rules</code>).</sub>

> Note how one physical event can trigger several rules. The pH jump at sample 60 raises ROC-001 both on the way up and on the way back down. The CON-001 injection also raises ROC-001 for TDS. That's intended: the engine reports every rule a record breaks, and the engineer sees the full picture.

### 12.8 Reference implementation (excerpt)

The full file is [`reference/edge/verification_engine.py`](reference/edge/verification_engine.py). Key excerpt:

```python
class VerificationEngine:
    """Stateful per-node verification engine (keeps short history per node)."""

    def verify(self, msg, received_at=None) -> VerificationResult:
        received_at = received_at or datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        hits: List[RuleHit] = []

        # FMT: format validation
        if not isinstance(msg, dict):
            return self._result(None, None, [RuleHit("FMT-001", None, Status.INVALID,
                                "Message is not a JSON object")], {}, received_at)
        missing = [f for f in REQUIRED_FIELDS if f not in msg]
        if missing:
            hits.append(RuleHit("FMT-002", None, Status.INVALID,
                                f"Missing required fields: {', '.join(missing)}"))
            return self._result(msg.get("record_id"), msg.get("node_id"), hits, msg, received_at)
        ...
        # RNG: sensor operating range (NOT a water-safety limit)
        lo, hi = self.cfg["ranges"][p]
        if v < lo or v > hi:
            hits.append(RuleHit("RNG-001", p, Status.SUSPECTED_SENSOR_ERROR,
                                f"{p}={v} outside sensor operating range [{lo}, {hi}]"))
        ...
        # ROC: rate of change, normalised per minute
        rate = abs(v - pv) / minutes
        if rate > self.cfg["max_rate_per_min"][p]:
            hits.append(RuleHit("ROC-001", p, Status.ABNORMAL, ...))
        ...
        return self._result(msg["record_id"], msg["node_id"], hits, msg, received_at)
```

### 12.9 Example result

```json
{
  "record_id": "SWN-0001-00001-00000061",
  "node_id": "SWN-0001",
  "verified_at": "2026-09-01T05:00:03+00:00",
  "status": "ABNORMAL",
  "verification": "VERIFICATION_FAILED",
  "rules_triggered": [
    { "rule_id": "ROC-001", "parameter": "ph", "status": "ABNORMAL",
      "reason": "ph changed 0.54/min (limit 0.5)" }
  ],
  "raw_snapshot": { "ph": 9.9, "turbidity_ntu": 5.12, "tds_ppm": 214.6,
                    "ec_us_cm": 431.0, "temperature_c": 26.83 },
  "raw_sha256": "3f1c…e9a0",
  "engine_version": "0.1.0"
}
```

### 12.10 Engine versioning & re-verification

- Every result stores `engine_version` and `config_hash`.
- When the rules or thresholds change, historical records **may be re-verified**. This creates **new** result rows (`<record_id>#v0.2.0`); the old results stay.
- The dashboard shows the result from the **currently active** engine version by default, and the history view can show all versions.

---

## 13. Data Integrity & Tamper Resistance

### 13.1 Permissions by role

| Action | ENGINEER (on-site) | SERVICE (debugger) | SUPERVISOR | ADMIN | DEVICE (edge) |
|---|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
| View live data, alerts, diagnostics, history | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | — |
| Acknowledge alerts | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | — |
| Log approved maintenance action | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | — |
| Run diagnostic commands | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | — |
| Start calibration session | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | — |
| Change configuration (thresholds, intervals) | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ (audited) | — |
| File correction annotation (audit event) | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ (audited, reason required) | — |
| Create raw readings | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ (create only) |
| **Modify historical raw readings** | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| **Delete readings** | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| **Rewrite timestamps** | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| **Change verification results manually** | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| **Alter audit records** | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |

> Not even ADMIN can edit raw data. The only path is an **annotation** in the audit log.

### 13.2 Enforcement layers

| Layer | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| UI | Edit/delete controls don't exist for raw, results or audit |
| Firebase Auth | Custom claims `role ∈ {engineer, service, supervisor, admin}` |
| Firestore Security Rules | `allow update, delete: if false` on `readings`, `results`, `audit`; create only by the device service account (Admin SDK) or by a Cloud Function |
| Cloud Functions | Audit entries written server-side; the client can't forge `actor_uid` or `ts` |
| Edge SQLite | Append-only triggers |
| Edge OS | Services run as unprivileged `swedge`; DB file owned by `swedge`, mode `0640` |
| Cryptographic | Per-node hash chain on raw records; per-gateway hash chain on the audit log |

### 13.3 Audit event schema

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `audit_id` | UUIDv7 |
| `ts` | Server timestamp (cloud) or edge UTC timestamp |
| `actor_uid` | Authenticated user ID (never free text) |
| `actor_role` | Role at the time of the action |
| `action` | e.g. `ALERT_ACK`, `CONFIG_CHANGE`, `CORRECTION_ANNOTATION`, `CALIBRATION_APPLIED`, `NODE_STATE_CHANGE`, `MAINTENANCE_LOGGED`, `LOGIN_FAILED` |
| `target_ref` | Path or ID of the affected object |
| `previous_value` | JSON snapshot before |
| `new_value` | JSON snapshot after |
| `reason` | Required, free text (minimum length enforced) |
| `prev_hash`, `hash` | Chain values |

### 13.4 Hash chain

<p align="center"><img src="diagrams/25_hash_chain.png" alt="Hash chain" width="100%"/></p>

<sub>Figure 13.1: Tamper evidence via a per-node hash chain.</sub>

```python
# swedge/storage/chain.py
import hashlib, json

GENESIS = "0" * 64

def canonical(obj) -> bytes:
    return json.dumps(obj, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")).encode()

def raw_hash(message: dict) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(canonical(message)).hexdigest()

def chain_hash(prev_chain: str, raw_sha256: str) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(bytes.fromhex(prev_chain) + bytes.fromhex(raw_sha256)).hexdigest()

def verify_chain(rows) -> tuple[bool, str | None]:
    """rows ordered by (boot_id, seq). Returns (ok, first_bad_record_id)."""
    prev = GENESIS
    for r in rows:
        if raw_hash(json.loads(r["payload_json"])) != r["raw_sha256"]:
            return False, r["record_id"]
        if r["prev_chain_hash"] != prev or chain_hash(prev, r["raw_sha256"]) != r["chain_hash"]:
            return False, r["record_id"]
        prev = r["chain_hash"]
    return True, None
```

The debugger's **`verify chain`** command runs `verify_chain()` over a node's history and reports the first broken link, if there is one. Periodically publishing the latest chain head to Firestore (`nodes/{id}.chain_head`) anchors the local chain to a second location.

> **Honest limitation.** A hash chain makes tampering *detectable*; it doesn't make it *impossible*. An attacker with full control of both the edge device and the cloud project could rebuild a consistent chain. Anchoring chain heads to an independent store is a future option ([§50](#50-future-scalability)).
